</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest pattern number} &= \dfrac {99} {4} \\\\
                              &= 24 \text{ remainder } 3 \\\\
                              &\approx 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

**Questions** $\bold{36}$ **to** $\bold{38}$ **relate to the triangle numbers**.

The Fibonacci numbers are a sequence of numbers formed so that the first two
numbers are $0$ and $1$, and each subsequent number is the sum of the two previous numbers.

So the first eight terms of the sequence are:

$0, 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13$

What is the next Fibonacci number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Next number}          &= 8 + 13 \\\\
                            &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Next number}          &= 8 + 13 \\\\
                            &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$21$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The fourteenth Fibonacci number is $377$ and the fifteenth Fibonacci number is
$610$.

What is the sixteenth Fibonacci number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
16^\text{th} \text { number}        &= 377 + 610 \\\\
                                    &= 987
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$987$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
16^\text{th} \text { number}        &= 377 + 610 \\\\
                                    &= 987
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$987$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Write down the next fraction in the sequence: $\dfrac{5}{8}, \quad \dfrac{10}{16}, \quad \dfrac{15}{24}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


$
\begin{matrix}
    \footnotesize{\maroonC{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{1} {1} )}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{2} {2} )}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{3} {3} )}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{4} {4} )}} \\\\
\dfrac {5} {8},  &&    \dfrac {10} {16},  &&  \dfrac {15} {24},   && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}       &= \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{4} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {8 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {20} {32}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {20} {32}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
    \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{1} {1} )}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{2} {2} )}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{3} {3} )}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{( \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{4} {4} )}} \\\\
\dfrac {5} {8},  &&    \dfrac {10} {16},  &&  \dfrac {15} {24},   && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Missing number}       &= \dfrac {5} {8} \times \dfrac{4} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {8 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {20} {32}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $3$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}  \\
10, && 13, && 16, && \ldots,&& \ldots, && 25, && 28
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 16 + 3 \\\\
                                  &= 19 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}      &= 19 + 3 \\\\
                                  &= 22
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$22$

</div>

&   \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}

&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+3)}}  \\

\end{matrix}

                                  &= 19 \\\\

                                  &= 22
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

A pattern is made using black and white squares. The first four patterns are shown below:

![latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-00.png](/assets/latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-00.png "latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-00.png")    ![latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-01.png](/assets/latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-01.png "latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-01.png")    ![latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-02.png](/assets/latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-02.png "latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-02.png")    ![latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-03.png](/assets/latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-03.png "latymer-upper-school--11-plus--maths--2014--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-28-03.png")

</div>

| Number of black squares     | $0$     | $3$     |         |         |

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many black squares will there be in the $8^{th}$ pattern?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of black squares} &= \dfrac{8}{2} \times 3 \\\\
                               &= \dfrac{4 \times 2}{2} \times 3 \\\\
                               &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \times 3 \\\\
                               &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
                               &= 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of black squares} &= \dfrac{8}{2} \times 3 \\\\
                               &= \dfrac{4 \times 2}{2} \times 3 \\\\
                               &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \times 3 \\\\
                               &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
                               &= 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many squares will there be in total in the $19^{th}$ pattern?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of squares is calculated by adding $3$ to the previous number of squares.

This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next pattern is made by adding multiple of $3$ squares to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of squares in } 19^\text{th} \text{ pattern}   &= 1 + (19 - 1) \times 3 \\\\
                                                            &= 1 + 18 \times 3 \\\\
                                                            &= 1 + 54 \\\\
                                                            &= 55 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The number of squares is calculated by adding $3$ to the previous number of squares.
<emptyline>
This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next pattern is made by adding multiple of $3$ squares to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of squares in } 19^\text{th} \text{ pattern}   &= 1 + (19 - 1) \times 3 \\\\
                                                            &= 1 + 18 \times 3 \\\\
                                                            &= 1 + 54 \\\\
                                                            &= 55 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$55$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which pattern number has $31$ white squares and $33$ black squares?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the term number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
1 + \dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3 &= 31 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3     &= 31 - 1 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3     &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= \dfrac{30}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= \dfrac{3 \times 10}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= \dfrac{\cancel{3} \times 10}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= 10 \\\\
a - 1                           &= 10 \times 2 \\\\
a - 1                           &= 20 \\\\
a                               &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
0 + \dfrac{a}{2} \times 3    &= 33 \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= \dfrac{33}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= \dfrac{11 \times 3}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= \dfrac{11 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= 11 \\\\
a                            &= 11 \times 2 \\\\
a                            &= 22
\end{aligned}
$

$22^\text{nd}$ pattern number has $31$ white squares and $33$ black squares.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$22$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the term number $= a$
<emptyline>

\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= 10 \\\\
a - 1                           &= 10 \times 2 \\\\
a - 1                           &= 20 \\\\
a                               &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
0 + \dfrac{a}{2} \times 3    &= 33 \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= \dfrac{33}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= \dfrac{11 \times 3}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= \dfrac{11 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2}                 &= 11 \\\\
a                            &= 11 \times 2 \\\\
a                            &= 22
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$22^\text{nd}$ pattern number has $31$ white squares and $33$ black squares.
</code></pre>

</code></pre>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sam draws a pattern like the ones above and says, "there are 96 black squares and $95$ white squares in my pattern." How can you tell that he must have miscounted?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the term number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
1 + \dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3 &= 95 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3     &= 95 - 1 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3     &= 94 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= \dfrac{94}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= 31.33 \text{ (Not possible)}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Not possible

</div>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the term number $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1 + \dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3 &= 95 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3     &= 95 - 1 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2} \times 3     &= 94 \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= \dfrac{94}{3} \\\\
\dfrac{(a - 1)}{2}              &= 31.33 \text{ (Not possible)}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Bob makes a sequence using the following rule: ‘double and subtract $3$’. If the first number in his sequence is $5$, what will the fifth number in his sequence be?

- $157$ 

- $77$ 

- $19$ 

- $35$ 

- $67$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

| Sequence          | Value            |
|:---------------:|:----------------------:|
| $1$             | $5$                    |
| $2$             | $5 \times 2 - 3 = 7$   |
| $3$             | $7 \times 2 - 3 = 11$  |
| $4$             | $11 \times 2 - 3 = 19$ |
| $5$             | $19 \times 2 - 3 = 35$ |

Fifth number $= 35$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$35$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>| Sequence          | Value            |
|:---------------:|:----------------------:|
| $1$             | $5$                    |
| $2$             | $5 \times 2 - 3 = 7$   |
| $3$             | $7 \times 2 - 3 = 11$  |
| $4$             | $11 \times 2 - 3 = 19$ |
| $5$             | $19 \times 2 - 3 = 35$ |
<emptyline>
Fifth number $= 35$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

$7, 14, 21, 28,$ _______, _______.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding $7$ to the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+7)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+7)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+7)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+7)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+7)} }  \\
7, && 14, && 21, && 28, && \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 28 + 7 \\\\
                                  &= 35 \\\\
\text{Second missing number}      &= 35 + 7 \\\\
                                  &= 42
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>

Look at the patterns of grey and white tiles:

![leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-00.png](/assets/leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-00.png "leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-00.png")   ![leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-01.png](/assets/leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-01.png "leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-01.png")   ![leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-02.png](/assets/leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-02.png "leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-18-02.png")

Complete the following table for patterns $1$ to $3$ and extend the table for patterns $4$ and $5$.

| Pattern number     | Number of grey  tiles     | Number of white tiles     |
|:--------------:    |:---------------------:    |:---------------------:    |
|       $1$          |          $1$              |          $8$              |
|       $2$          |          $2$              |                           |
|       $3$          |                           |                           |
|       $4$          |                           |                           |
|       $5$          |                           |                           |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of white tiles is calculated by adding $2$ to the previous number of white tiles.

The number of grey tiles is equal to pattern number.

| Pattern number | Number of grey tiles | Number of white tiles |
|:--------------:|:--------------------:|:---------------------:|
| $1$            | $1$                  | $8$                   |
| $2$            | $2$                  | $10$                  |
| $3$            | $3$                  | $12$                  |
| $4$            | $4$                  | $14$                  |
| $5$            | $5$                  | $16$                  |

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prrl question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>04045CC1-64EE-467C-8FD5-97F0AF415F0E</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3:1:20</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prrl</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsequence
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Patterns are designed on a sheet of wallpaper so that every white square is surrounded by four coloured squares. Two of the possible arrangements are shown below.

Pattern A is shown below for $1,\:\: 2$ and $3$ white squares.

![leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-20-00.png](/assets/leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-20-00.png "leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-20-00.png")

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many coloured squares are needed when $6$ white squares are used? 

Pattern B is shown below for $1,\:\: 2$ and $3$ white squares.

![leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-20-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-20-subquestion-1-00.png "leicester-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-3/section-1-question-20-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of coloured squares is calculated by adding $3$ to previous number of coloured squares.

The number of white squares is equal to the term number.

This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next pattern is made by adding multiple of $3$ coloured squares to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

The term number $= 6$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of coloured squares}   &= 4 + (6 - 1) \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 4 + 5 \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 4 + 15 \\\\
                                    &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The number of coloured squares is calculated by adding $3$ to previous number of coloured squares.
<emptyline>
The number of white squares is equal to the term number.
<emptyline>
This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next pattern is made by adding multiple of $3$ coloured squares to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
The term number $= 6$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of coloured squares}   &= 4 + (6 - 1) \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 4 + 5 \times 3 \\\\
                                    &= 4 + 15 \\\\
                                    &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\text{Twenty}$ coloured squares are used in Pattern B. How many white squares are needed?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of coloured squares is calculated by adding $2$ to previous number of coloured squares.

The number of white squares is equal to the term number.

This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next pattern is made by adding multiple of $2$ coloured squares to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

Let the term number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 + (a - 1) \times 2       &= 20 \\\\
4 + 2a - 2                 &= 20 \\\\
2a + 2                     &= 20 \\\\
2a                         &= 20 - 2 \\\\
2a                         &= 18 \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{18}{2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{9 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of white squares}   &= a \\\\
                                 &= 9 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The number of coloured squares is calculated by adding $2$ to previous number of coloured squares.
<emptyline>
The number of white squares is equal to the term number.
<emptyline>
This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next pattern is made by adding multiple of $2$ coloured squares to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
Let the term number $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 + (a - 1) \times 2       &= 20 \\\\
4 + 2a - 2                 &= 20 \\\\
2a + 2                     &= 20 \\\\
2a                         &= 20 - 2 \\\\
2a                         &= 18 \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{18}{2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{9 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of white squares}   &= a \\\\
                                 &= 9 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

A wheel has $3$ different colours painted on its rim, red (R), green (G) and yellow (Y).

As it turns, a pattern is left on the ground.

![north-london-collegiate-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-33-00.png](/assets/north-london-collegiate-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-33-00.png "north-london-collegiate-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-33-00.png")

The first mark is yellow, and the second mark is red, so the pattern left on the ground will be:

Y R G Y R ... 

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the colour of the $20^{th}$ mark?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{20}{3} \\\\
&= 6 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

colour of the $20^{th}$ mark = R

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

R

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{20}{3} \\\\
&= 6 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
colour of the $20^{th}$ mark = R
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>R
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Another wheel makes the pattern:

G Y Y R G Y Y R G ...

What is the colour of the $38^{th}$ mark?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{38}{4} \\\\
&= 9 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

colour of the $38^{th}$ mark = Y

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

Another wheel has $6$ **different** colours. No colour is repeated.

If red is the $83^{rd}$ colour in the pattern, how many colours are at the start of the pattern before red occurs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{83}{6} \\\\
&= 13 \text{ remainder } 5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$4$ colours are at the start of the pattern before red occurs

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by multiplying the previous term by $\dfrac{x}{3}$. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC { (\times \dfrac{x}{3})} }   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC { (\times \dfrac{x}{3})} }
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC { (\times \dfrac{x}{3})} }
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC { (\times \dfrac{x}{3})} } 
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC { (\times \dfrac{x}{3})} }   
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC { (\times \dfrac{x}{3})} } \\

\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}     &= \dfrac{x^5}{81} \times \dfrac{x}{3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{x^5 \times x}{81 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{x^6}{243} \\\\
\text{Second missing number}    &= \dfrac{x^6}{243} \times \dfrac{x}{3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{x^6 \times x}{243 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac{x^7}{729}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{x^6}{243}$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which SHAPE number will have a total of $36$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The Total is equal to the sum of natural number upto shape number.

| Shape number   | Total                                |
|:--------------:|:------------------------------------:|
| $1$            | $1$                                  |
| $2$            | $1 + 2 = 3$                          |
| $3$            | $1 + 2 + 3 = 6$                      |
| $4$            | $1 + 2 + 3 + 4 = 10$                 |
| $5$            | $1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5 = 15$             |
| $6$            | $1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 6 = 21$         |
| $7$            | $1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 6 + 7 = 28$     |
| $8$            | $1 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 6 + 7 + 8 = 36$ |

As per the table,

$8$ SHAPE number will have a total of $36$

                            &= 512
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which pattern has $117$ balls?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the pattern count $= p$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 + 4 \times (p-1)  &= 117 \\\\
1 + 4p - 4          &= 117 \\\\
4p                  &= 117 + 4 - 1 \\\\
4p                  &= 120 \\\\
p                   &= \dfrac{120}{4} \\\\
p                   &= \dfrac{30 \times 4}{4} \\\\
p                   &= \dfrac{30 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
p                   &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Number of pattern $= 30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the pattern count $= p$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}

4p                  &= 120 \\\\
p                   &= \dfrac{120}{4} \\\\
p                   &= \dfrac{30 \times 4}{4} \\\\
p                   &= \dfrac{30 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
p                   &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of pattern $= 30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

What is the $100^\text{th}$ symbol in this endless string of symbols?

$\&\pounds=\&\pounds=\&\pounds=\&\pounds=\&\pounds=$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

The pattern is repeating in a set of $3$. The first symbol is $\&$, second is $\pounds$ and third is $=$.

If we divide $100$ by $3$, the remainder is $1$. So the symbol in the first position will repeat in the $100 ^ \text{th}$ position.

$100 ^ \text{th}$ symbol $= \&$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\&$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>The pattern is repeating in a set of $3$. The first symbol is $\&$, second is $\pounds$ and third is $=$.
<emptyline>
If we divide $100$ by $3$, the remainder is $1$. So the symbol in the first position will repeat in the $100 ^ \text{th}$ position.
<emptyline>
$100 ^ \text{th}$ symbol $= \&$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\&$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Write down the next two numbers in the sequence:

$14, \quad 10, \quad 6, \quad 2, \quad \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore }, \quad \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore }$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by subtracting $4$ from the previous term. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin {matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(-4)}}  \\
14,  &&    10,  &&  6,  &&   2,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end {matrix}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First missing number}       &= 2 - 4 \\\\
                                   &= -2 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}      &= -2 - 4 \\\\
                                   &= -6
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$-2$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-6$

$
\begin {matrix}
    \footnotesize{\maroonC{(1^2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(2^2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(3^2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(4^2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(5^2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(6^2)}}  \\\\

\end {matrix}

\begin {aligned}
\text {First missing number}       &= 5^2 \\\\
                                   &= 25 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}      &= 6^2 \\\\
                                   &= 36
\end {aligned}

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


$
\begin {matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+3)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+5)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+7)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+9)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{(+11)}}  \\
2,  &&    5,  &&  10,  &&   17,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end {matrix}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {First missing number}       &= 17 + 9 \\\\
                                   &= 26 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}      &= 26 + 11 \\\\
                                   &= 37
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$37$

</div>

&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+5)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+7)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+9)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{(+11)}}  \\
2,  &&    5,  &&  10,  &&   17,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end {matrix}
$

\text {First missing number}       &= 17 + 9 \\\\
                                   &= 26 \\\\

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Write down the next two numbers in the sequence:

$1, -2, 4, -8, \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore },\text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore }$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by multiplying the previous term by $-2$. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize{\maroonC{\times (-2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{\times (-2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{\times (-2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{\times (-2)}}
&&  \footnotesize{\maroonC{\times (-2)}}  \\
1,  &&    -2,  &&  4,  &&   -8,  &&   \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {First missing number}   &= -8 \times -2 \\\\
                               &= 16 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}  &= 16 \times -2 \\\\
                               &= -32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>

&&  \footnotesize{\rtq_katex_color_info{\times (-2)}}  \\

\end{matrix}
$

$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
